# Project memory

Standing conventions and corrections, recorded as they came up. One memory
per file in `memory/`, indexed here. New memories go in this directory and
this index - not into any per-user directory - so the project stays the single
home of its own memory.

- [No mocks, scenario tests only](memory/no-mocks-scenario-tests.md) — every test uses real Scryfall cards through the Scenario harness; mocks are banned
- [Never recall MTG rules from memory](memory/never-recall-mtg-rules-from-memory.md) — every rule and CR number must be verified with knowledge_search; training data is wrong
- [magic-cards-model is a rules engine](memory/magic-cards-model-engine-goal.md) — the target is playable PvP MTG, not a card database
- [Subagents on the cheaper model](memory/subagents-on-cheaper-model.md) — pass model: "sonnet" on every Agent dispatch unless told otherwise
- [Engine fixes land in the engine](memory/engine-fixes-in-engine-only.md) — no API/frontend workarounds for rules gaps; only the most correct engine implementation
- [User examples become test cases](memory/user-examples-become-testcases.md) — every play-testing example the user reports is converted to a scenario test
- [Restart the API server after fixes](memory/restart-api-server-after-fixes.md) — uvicorn caches code at boot; kill by port (lsof -ti :8000), and verify the serving process predates no fix
- [Silent-default dispatch tables](memory/silent-default-dispatch-tables.md) — every defect class the loop has found is a lookup that defaults instead of refusing; fail closed and shout on a miss
- [Effect registry is the dominant gap](memory/effect-registry-is-the-dominant-gap.md) — most cards resolve and do nothing; the measured survey, per-deck dead-card counts, and the ranked order of work
- [Verify agent findings against the engine](memory/verify-agent-findings-against-the-engine.md) — adjudicators and reviewers each produce confident wrong claims; reproduce before acting
- [Harness deadlock defaulted every decision](memory/harness-deadlock-defaulted-every-decision.md) — 18 decisions per game timed out unanswered; re-check past "engine did nothing" reports
- [Autonomous playtest loop](memory/autonomous-playtest-loop.md) — play, report, confirm with opus, fix globally with sonnet, repeat until three clean games per deck pair
- [No commits: git checkout destroys work](memory/no-commits-git-checkout-destroys-work.md) — this repo has zero commits, so git restore/checkout/stash overwrite with an ancient index blob; recover from ~/.claude/file-history
- [Agent playtest launch](memory/agent-playtest-launch.md) — two AI players as Agent-tool subagents driving seat_cli.py: sonnet, one-session prompts, no curl (seat hides the opponent's hand), vet their reports against the CR
- [Combat declarations skip the live provider](memory/combat-declarations-skip-the-live-provider.md) — resolved 2026-09-07 (T8) by deleting DecisionScript.provider entirely, not by wiring it up; every asker in machines/decisions.py now answers one way (script, tape, pause, default)
- [Delayed trigger serialization vs. a game.py isolation lock](memory/delayed-trigger-serialization-game-py-isolation.md) — DelayedTrigger.effect became a discriminated union while game.py was locked; a narrow before-validator plus singleton-named specs bridge its 3 legacy call sites instead of blocking
- [KeywordActivation round trip and object identity](memory/keyword-activation-round-trip-identity.md) — typing AbilityOnStack.keyword_activation/.unless_payment correctly still left a decoded KeywordActivation.card as a copy, not the battlefield object; _equip_effect mutated the copy until it re-resolved by id
- [could_pay_mana_cost predicate](memory/could-pay-mana-cost-predicate.md) — the pure affordability predicate could not build a CostPayment at all (for_total registers a pool into open_payment_pools); _SpendContext duck-types its three read-only fields instead, and Card became frozen=True to make the new ability-parse caches sound
- [Flow-based auto-tap source grouping](memory/flow-based-auto-tap-source-grouping.md) — max-flow replaced auto-tap's exponential search, but only after grouping a permanent's several abilities into one provider node; two independent nodes for Eldrazi Temple's {C}/{C}{C} let capacities sum (1+2=3 from one land, one {T})
- [Legal actions enumeration](memory/legal-actions-enumeration.md) — legal_actions never gates on affordability (CR 118.6); why_cannot_cast_now waved a land through with no type check (now refused at the source, Casting itself, CR 305.1); hoisting _auto_tap_candidates needed a spend-independent/spend-dependent split, not just a cache; PERMISSION/STATIC alt-costs are ported into Game.alternative_costs_for and a targeted off-battlefield activation (Boseiju's Channel) reports UNKNOWN instead of vanishing — both were wrongly deferred first pass, fixed second pass
- [Tap plan reported, not discarded](memory/tap-plan-reported-not-discarded.md) — the auto-tap solve ran twice per action and was thrown away both times; plan_mana_payment returns it, could_pay_mana_cost became `is not None`, and Action.pays_with reports it - the ability index is read back off the hoisted candidate scan because re-deriving it per entry cost 12% of a legal_actions call
- [Game.schema_version fails closed](memory/game-schema-version-fail-closed.md) — a required no-default field plus a mode="before" validator refuses a mismatched or missing version; the exception subclasses RuntimeError, not ValueError, because pydantic-core rewraps ValueError into a generic ValidationError and would swallow it
- [Scenario round-trip/replay invariant](memory/scenario-round-trip-replay-invariant.md) — every scenario now proves it round-trips and replays; found ~30 Game/token/keyword methods minting ids with no `_id_allocator_scope` open (ids.py's own list was incomplete), an unseeded `random.Random(None)` in cascade's bottoming, a copy-of-a-permanent-spell that never actually became a TokenState, and DecisionScript.provider crashing model_dump with no exclude=True; a global default-allocator design was tried and reverted for breaking legal_actions' purity guarantee
- [Callables became discriminated unions](memory/callables-became-discriminated-unions.md) — 2026-09-07. A keyed registry still defaults silently on an unknown key at decode; a discriminated union on `kind` refuses instead, and carries the closure's own captured parameters as typed fields, which a string key never could
- [Pause is a snapshot plus a tape](memory/pause-is-snapshot-plus-tape.md) — 2026-09-07. Not a `pending` field on `Game`: a half-applied mid-resolution game is not a legal state to persist, so a paused game is (last committed Game, the command, the answers so far) and resuming re-derives the identical decision via ids.py's deterministic allocator
- [Announcement is atomic](memory/announcement-is-atomic.md) — 2026-09-07. `Casting.model_post_init` runs every refusal (CR 305.1, CR 117.1a, restrictions) before CR 601.2a ever moves the card to the stack, so an illegal cast is refused with a reason instead of half-applied
- [DecisionPending is a BaseException](memory/decision-pending-is-a-baseexception.md) — 2026-09-07. Measured: 3 real `except Exception:` sites and 42 real `except ValueError:` sites in `src/` around effect resolution; any of them catching an `Exception`-derived pause would silently turn it into "the effect did nothing", so it derives from `BaseException` instead
- [Exile is a shared zone](memory/exile-is-a-shared-zone.md) — 2026-09-07. `player.add(state, Zone.EXILE)` builds a per-player exile pile the rules have no name for (CR 400.1, CR 406.2); it is invisible to `Game.exile`, to `all_targetables`, and to every seat's board, and `Player.cards_in(Zone.EXILE)` hides it from tests
- [Exile is a shared zone: the full sweep](memory/exile-is-a-shared-zone.md) — 2026-09-08. Nine sites, not five (cascade, Possibility Storm, Wish, Hex Magic, Interdimensional Web Watch and Gemstone Caverns wrote into a per-player pile; three more read one). `Player.zones` is now a `PlayerZones` and `cards_in`/`add`/`move` raise `SharedZoneError` (CR 400.1) instead of minting the pile a read used to create, which is what hid every instance from its own tests — the api's `_all_targetables` had the same defect
- Leaves-the-battlefield triggers fired on one route in five — 2026-09-08. `Game.put_into_graveyard` emits `LEAVES_THE_BATTLEFIELD` *before* moving the card; `exile_permanent`, `return_to_hand`, `put_cards_into_library`, and `ante` all emit it *after*. `machines/triggers._LOOK_BACK_EVENTS` (CR 603.10a's own look-back list) did not name the event, so `zones_ability_functions_in`'s plain-battlefield default only ever matched the pre-move emit — the graveyard route worked by accident, the other four were silently refused. No scenario test anywhere had sent a *printed* leaves-the-battlefield ability through a non-graveyard route; two tests that looked like coverage (`test_trigger_events.py`'s exile/hand pair) only proved `Game.emit` raises the event, through a `DelayedTrigger` watcher that bypasses the zone gate entirely. Fixed by adding `TriggerEvent.LEAVES_THE_BATTLEFIELD` to `_LOOK_BACK_EVENTS`. `put_cards_into_library` also had no `_id_allocator_scope` at all — a second, unrelated pre-existing defect the new tuck-route test exposed via the round-trip/replay invariant, fixed alongside it
- Damage to a planeswalker was a missing *damage result*, not a missing state-based action - 2026-09-08. The report blamed "the damage-to-loyalty state-based action"; there is no such action. CR 120.3c / CR 306.8 remove the loyalty counters as a **result of the damage event itself**, immediately, and only the graveyard move (CR 306.9 / CR 704.5i) is a state-based action - and that half already worked, which is why the walker sat at `loyalty=9` through every subsequent priority pass looking like an SBA that never fired. The real cause was one line: `Game.deal_damage` ended `else: target.mark_damage(...)` for every non-player target, so a planeswalker collected marked damage (`dmg:9` on the board line) that no rule reads. CR 120.3 is a list of results that "may have one or more" apply, not a dispatch table, so the fix is per-type branches with no fallback: planeswalker -> loyalty counters off, creature -> damage marked, both -> both, neither -> nothing. Two sites, not one: `machines/effects.damage_the_attacked_player` (Hellrider) *also* returned early on a planeswalker defender, citing the same missing rule, so the fix in `deal_damage` alone would have left the card still half-dead
- [A substring guard inverted a whole class of triggers](memory/nontoken-substring-guard-inverted-triggers.md) — 2026-09-08. `"nontoken"` contains `"token"`, so a plain `"token" in lowered` guard demanding the entering permanent be a token matched "nontoken artifact"/"nontoken Dragon" too and inverted Weapons Manufacturing's and Miirym's triggers; a word-boundary test alone stops the false match but does not enforce the restriction. `scripts/sweep_pool.py` never calls `condition_matches`, so a trigger whose condition can never match still scores `handled`
- [The sweeper measured effects, never trigger registration](memory/sweeper-measured-effects-not-trigger-registration.md) — 2026-09-08. `scripts/sweep_pool.py` never called `condition_matches`, so a trigger whose condition can never match still scored `handled` (Weapons Manufacturing and Miirym, both fixed on `main` by the nontoken substring fix above) and a fallback parser's answer could come from inside a quoted span rather than the clause's own text; two new closures (`_condition_can_fire`, `_effect_matched_inside_quotes`) catch both, tracked as buckets distinct from `silent`. Ygra, Eater of All and Blade of the Bloodchief hit a different, still-open gap in `condition_matches`'s `DIES` branch: a condition naming neither a self-reference nor "another"/"other" never matches anything
- Into the Flood Maw's "gift was promised" clause was two independent defects, not one - 2026-09-09. `machines/targeting.py`'s `_parse` counted the card's own conditional replacement clause ("If the gift was promised, instead return target nonland permanent ...") as a *second* required target, so a legal single-target cast was refused with "too few targets: 1 chosen where the spell requires 2" (CR 601.2c actually says a spell has alternative targets "only if" the alternative cost was chosen - never both at once, CR 702.174m). Fixing only that left the card castable but inert: `machines/effects.py`'s `_parse_mixed_clauses` had no shape for the second sentence either, so the whole card resolved with no effect at all, silently. Both needed their own fix - `TargetRequirement.if_gift_promised` / `TargetingProfile.for_gift_promise` (targeting) and `_parse_gift_promised_return_instead_clause` (effects, mirroring the existing Fatal Push Revolt-line precedent: the conditional half is enforced by the targeting layer, not re-checked at resolution). Gift itself is still not a payable additional cost (`ADDITIONAL_COST_KEYWORDS` has no "gift" entry), so the promised branch is unreachable from a real cast and is covered by a strict `xfail` in `tests/scenarios/test_gift_targeting.py` rather than a passing scenario
- "As though it had haste" is not the haste keyword — 2026-09-09. A seat piloting Devoted Combo could not tap a summoning-sick Devoted Druid under Tyvar, Jubilant Brawler and filed it under the "Timing restrictions on an activated ability" gap (CR 602.5b/d/e). Wrong bucket: that row is fail-open timing, and the real gap was that nothing read Tyvar's static at all. CR 609.4 - "This applies only to the stated effect" - is what makes the fix narrow: the stated effect is *activating abilities*, so only CR 702.10c's clause is borrowed and CR 702.10b's attacking clause is not, and the creature never gains the keyword, so `machines/combat.has_keyword` and `can_attack` must stay untouched. Granting haste in layer 6 instead would have been the plausible-and-wrong fix: it would have let the Druid attack. Read as a shape at `Game.activation_haste_permission`, the permission twin of `_activation_prohibition`, with `_record_unread_prohibition` renamed `_record_unread_static_line` because a permission read as silence is the same class of defect as a prohibition read as permission
- A blinked target's stable `id` hid a CR 400.7 zone-change bypass of CR 608.2b — 2026-09-09. Erode plus Ephemerate, confirmed live: a removal spell's sole target was exiled and returned while the removal sat on the stack, and it resolved anyway and killed the (new) object - the graveyard card carried the *same* `CardState.id` it had before the blink, because this engine mutates one `CardState` in place across a zone change rather than replacing it. `TargetRef` had no record of which incarnation it named, so `Game.legal_target`'s `find_by_id` lookup could never tell the two apart. Fix: `TargetRef.zone_change_stamp` (optional UUID, filled from the live `CardState.zone_change_stamp` inside `TargetRef.to_card`/`coerce` - the same idiom `layers.only_this_incarnation`/`DelayedTrigger.expected_stamp` already use), and `Game.legal_target` rejects a card whose current stamp no longer matches a non-`None` one. Load-bearing trap: the stamp had to stay **out of** `TargetRef.__eq__`/`__hash__` (overridden to the `(kind, card_id, username)` triple only) or `Game._validate_answer`'s `CHOOSE_TARGETS` branch would refuse a legal answer built with no stamp - exactly `magic_cards_model_api.decisions.targets_answer`'s bare-id shape, which has no live `CardState` to read a stamp off. That branch now also swaps a caller's answer for its canonical, already-stamped `decision.targets` counterpart, or the fix would have been a no-op over the API surface the bug was reported from (proved live through the real `/decide` endpoint, not just the engine harness). One accepted, documented collateral: `tests/scenarios/test_cast_triggers.py::test_the_kicked_trigger_fires_and_exiles_the_target_land` - Sowing Mycospawn's two "when you cast this spell" triggers share one staged target list keyed by card name (`Game.trigger_targets`), so the land-fetch trigger (which prints no target of its own) inherits the exile trigger's now-stale reference and legitimately fizzles too; fixing the name-keying is a separate, deferred gap. `Game.CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped 3 -> 4
- "Return target `<quality>` card from your graveyard to the battlefield." had no reader — 2026-09-09. The bare singular sentence (no self-reference, no "all", no trailing decoration) was the one shape `_RETURN_FROM_YOUR_GRAVEYARD_TO_HAND_RE`'s battlefield twin never got; fixed with `_RETURN_FROM_YOUR_GRAVEYARD_TO_BATTLEFIELD_RE` / `return_ability_targets_to_battlefield`, closing Unearth, Renegade Rallier, and Tameshi, Reality Architect. Reanimating Renegade Rallier for real surfaced a second, independent gap: `machines/triggers.intervening_if_holds` had no reader for Revolt's own intervening-*if* wording ("a permanent left the battlefield under your control this turn") — Fatal Push's Revolt is read by `targeting.py`'s `comparison_value_if_revolt` because it modifies a target restriction, a completely different code path from a triggered ability's intervening if, so Renegade Rallier's ETB failed closed even with Revolt genuinely active. Fixed via `_REVOLT_CLAUSES`, sharing `Game.revolt_active_for`. `_MASS_RETURN_FROM_GRAVEYARD_TO_BATTLEFIELD_RE` gained an optional trailing " tapped" (CR 110.5b) for Aftermath Analyst. Persist (the sorcery, same five-card template) is deliberately not shipped: its "with a -1/-1 counter on it" is a CR 614.1c enters-with replacement, not add-after — recorded in `docs/engine-coverage.md`'s Not-modelled table instead. Separately verified and ruled *not* mine: a live-game report of Postmortem Lunge reading an announced X as 0 during targeting turned out to be a caller-ordering bug (choosing targets before announcing X) — `test_postmortem_lunge.py` already proves the engine's own `Casting` API is correct in CR 107.3a's order, and an activated ability (Tameshi included) can never hit this window at all since `x_value` and `targets` are announced in the same call.
- Echoing Deeps confirmed live: two `None`s collapsed into one default — 2026-09-09. `_parse_copy_exception` already returned `None` both for "no except clause at all" and for "a clause this parser cannot read", and every caller read it with `effect.copy_exception or CopyException()` - a fail-closed parser result treated as a fail-open default. Echoing Deeps' "it's a Cave in addition to its other types" has no power/toughness, which `_COPY_EXCEPT_PT_AND_TYPES` required, so the whole tail fell through and the land copied a graveyard land without ever becoming a Cave - confirmed by actually playing it (`subtypes=('Forest',)`, no Cave) before touching any code. Fixed by making the P/T half of that regex optional (Echoing Deeps now genuinely works) and by giving `ReplacementEffect` a `copy_exception_unparsed` field so a still-unreadable tail (Mockingbird's "... and it has flying", a granted ability) fails closed *loudly* - a new `UnreadableCopyExceptionNotice`, the whole "enter as a copy" replacement skipped - instead of silently defaulting. Blast radius: only 3 printings in the pool have an "as a copy of ... except" tail at all; Superior Spider-Man's was already fully readable and is untouched. Separate, out-of-scope defect found live in the same function and left unfixed: `_enters_as_copy`'s own `apply()` never reads `effect.tapped` at all, so "enter **tapped** as a copy of" never actually taps the permanent - noted, not fixed, since it is not the conflation bug shape this session was about
- The sweeper stamped a triggered ability SILENT the instant `ability.event is None`, without ever asking `triggers._unread_trigger_events`/`_STATE_COUNTER_TRIGGER_RE` whether the *runtime* could still read the condition — 2026-09-09. `triggered_abilities_of` and `_state_trigger_abilities` both already consult those two matchers before giving up, so ten clauses across nine cards that reach the stack in a real game (Basking Broodscale, Caged Sun, Emperor of Bones, Hardened Academic, Ketramose, Murktide Regent, Tamiyo Inquisitive Student, The Millennium Calendar, Wan Shi Tong) were being reported dead. Fix was the sweeper asking the same two questions before filing "triggered (unread condition)". Measured before/after with a real `sweep_pool.py --effects` run: 132 of 715 silent cards down to 128; the four with no other silent clause (Basking Broodscale, Emperor of Bones, Hardened Academic, Murktide Regent) left the SILENT list entirely, the other five moved to a different, still-genuine gap (a missing effect entry or an unaudited static) rather than staying miscounted. `tests/test_sweep_pool.py` now pins sweeper readability against the runtime's own gate directly, over the whole pool, so the two cannot drift apart again.
- "Each opponent's graveyard" was not read by either exile-graveyard shape — 2026-09-09. Soul-Guide Lantern's `{T}, Sacrifice this artifact: Exile each opponent's graveyard.` and Ashiok, Dream Render's `-1` both failed on that one phrase: `_EXILE_TARGET_PLAYERS_GRAVEYARD_RE` needs "target", and `_EXILE_ALL_GRAVEYARDS_RE` has no player-set qualifier at all. The dangerous wrong fix would have been routing the wording through `exile_all_graveyards` (unfiltered `game.players_in_game`) — that would exile the controller's own graveyard too, a board that looks correct and is not. `exile_each_opponents_graveyard` is `exile_all_graveyards`'s CR 102.2-filtered twin, reusing the existing `_opponents` helper (`damage_each_opponent`/Guttersnipe's own), dispatched via `_EXILE_EACH_OPPONENTS_GRAVEYARD_RE` (which keeps a leading "then " literal, the same way `_THEN_EXILE_THAT_PLAYERS_GRAVEYARD_RE` reads Go Blank's second clause, since Ashiok's own text splits into two sentences). Relic of Progenitus, Cling to Dust, and Lantern of Insight share neither shape and stay out of scope on purpose - Relic's "target player exiles a card from their graveyard" needs its own `CHOOSE_CARDS` decision, not this filter.
- Improvise was built and directly tested, but never reachable from a seat's own cast — 2026-09-09. A seat piloting Affinity could not cast Kappa Cannoneer for `{5}{U}` with a land plus five untapped artifacts (one of them Mox Opal), refused with "no combination of the available mana sources can pay {5}{U}" on both a real cast and the plain dry run. `machines/objects_as_costs.improvise`/`has_improvise` were real, tested end to end in `tests/scenarios/test_improvise.py`, and even cited correctly in `docs/engine-coverage.md` — but only ever driven directly against a hand-opened `CostPayment`. `models/game.py`'s `Game.cast`, the one atomic entry point `magic_cards_model_api` actually drives, imported `convoke`/`delve` and had no `improvise` parameter at all, and `Game.object_cost_offers` (read by `machines/legal_actions._widen_for_object_costs` to turn an affordability `NO` into an honest `UNKNOWN`) checked `has_convoke`/`has_delve`/`is_vehicle` and never `has_improvise` — so the machine was invisible on both the pay-it and the discover-it paths. Fixed by adding `improvise: Sequence[CardState] | None` to `Game.cast` (mirroring `delve`'s own shape exactly) and an `"improvise"` key to `object_cost_offers` (mirroring `"convoke"`/`"delve"`); no changes needed to `_widen_for_object_costs` itself, since it already reads `object_cost_offers` generically. The paired report that delve's own affordability check "does not evaluate delve at all" is not a separate bug: `_widen_for_object_costs`'s docstring already documents that as deliberate (an honest `UNKNOWN` beats replicating the reduction machinery a second time, which could disagree with what paying actually accepts), and improvise now gets the identical, correct treatment. This is the same "built but unwired" shape as the delayed-trigger and trigger-registration entries above — a real subsystem existing is not evidence a caller reaches it; check the seat-facing entry point, not just the machine.
- A readable clause killed by a rider nobody reads, and the two "fixes" that would have looked right — 2026-09-09. Two unrelated readers under one symptom (a strict prefix parses, the whole clause does not). (1) `_CLAUSE_SEPARATOR` eats "then" only as an infix, so a sentence-initial "Then" survived `split_effect_clauses` glued to the clause it introduces; stripping it after the split is sound because CR 608.2c's "in the order written" is all the word states. (2) An **object** anaphor ("It ...", "They ...", "... on it") reached no parser at all - `_ANAPHORIC_SUBJECT_RE` reads only player anaphors - so CR 608.2c's fail-closed guard correctly threw the readable half away, silently. Three traps, each of which produces a card that scores `handled` while doing the wrong thing: **never relax the guard** (Wrath of God would let regeneration through, Kaito would tap without the stun counters); **Ancient Stirrings looks fixed and is dead** without the second, unrelated fix to `_quality_word_matcher`, which read a colour word only as an adjective in front of a type, so bare "colorless" matched nothing and the card bottomed all five cards legally; and **do not widen `machines/layers._SUBJECT` to accept "it"** - `_is_layer_effect_text` is an any-sentence test over the whole ability, so The Wandering Emperor's entire `+1` would route to `create_continuous_effect_of_ability`, granting first strike and never placing the counter. The general mechanism is that the referent comes from the *antecedent's own subject phrase* (targets / a named group / CR 109.2's self-reference), not from the antecedent's parser, so every existing clause parser is reused untouched. Riders split in two: sequential ones act on the referent afterwards, and CR 608.2c's own worked example ("It can't be regenerated") *modifies* its antecedent instead, so it re-runs the destruction with the prohibition attached. Two subsystems had to be built for the riders not to be untestable no-ops - stun counters (CR 122.1d, inside `CardState.untap`) and regeneration shields (CR 701.19a, through `applicable_effects` so CR 616.1 orders them, with CR 701.19c's prohibition on the *event* rather than the permanent, since it skips a shield rather than spending it)
- Suspend's upkeep trigger had no dispatcher, and the api repo was papering over it — 2026-09-09. A seat suspended two Lotus Blooms and the time counters never ticked. Three independent blockers, all of which had to go: `Game.emit` built its source list out of the battlefield plus every graveyard and **never exile**, so no exiled card was ever asked about `TriggerEvent.BEGINNING_OF_STEP`; `triggers_for` reads `triggered_abilities_of(source.card)` and suspend's second ability is the keyword's expansion, not a printed line (Lotus Bloom's only matching string is reminder text), so no `TriggeredAbility` existed to find; and `zones_ability_functions_in` defaulted to BATTLEFIELD with madness's `EXILED` as its only EXILE branch. CR 702.62a names the fix shape itself - "The second and third are triggered abilities that function in the exile zone" - so both are keyword expansions now (`machines/keywords._suspend_abilities`, the registry widened to `list[TriggeredAbility]` per keyword) and both go on the stack, which is what makes CR 603.3b's APNAP ordering question get asked for two suspended cards and what lets a response outrun the tick. **The emit widening is CR 702.62b's own three clauses, not "exile"**: exile is one shared pile, and offering all of it makes every foretold, plotted, madness, cascade and hideaway card a source for every event - a Blood Artist card in exile then fires off a creature dying, since CR 603.10a's look-back events make `zones_ability_functions_in` answer `{BATTLEFIELD, source.zone}`. `CardState.is_suspended` and `finish_suspend` stopped reading `ExileMarker.SUSPENDED` at the same time: the marker records which action exiled the card and CR 702.62b asks nothing about that. "Your upkeep" is the **owner's** - `triggers.controller_of_source` now applies CR 108.4/108.4a by zone rather than trusting a stale `CardState.controller`, which `move_to` never clears. **The landing is not complete engine-side**: `magic-cards-model-api`'s `engine_api.drive_suspend_upkeep` (called from `commands._run_advance` and `PUT /api/games/{id}/step`) drives the same removal by hand and must be deleted in the same landing, or every upkeep ticks twice; the api suite proves it - `tests/test_special_actions.py::test_time_counters_come_off_each_upkeep_and_the_free_cast_reanimates` passes against unmodified `main` and fails against this engine with three orphaned suspend triggers stacked up under the free cast.
- Wrath of the Skies sparing an animated land was one bespoke parser, and a 68-site class behind it — 2026-09-09. The report blamed "the destroy effect selects on printed card types". Right in shape, wrong in scope both ways. **Narrower than reported**: the generic mass-destroy family (`_parse_mass_destroy_predicate` -> `_destroy_matching`) already read `game.characteristics_of`, and a Wrath of God test written against an earthbent land passed on the first run — only Wrath of the Skies had its own private loop (`_parse_x_energy_pay_destroy_by_mana_value`) filtering on `set(permanent.types)` and `permanent.mana_value`, twenty lines from a helper whose own docstring names "Wrath of the Skies's own 'artifact, creature, and enchantment'" as the grammar it reads. **Far wider than reported**: an audit of every `.types`/`.subtypes`/`.supertypes`/`.colors`/`.mana_value`/`.is_creature`/`.card.*` read in `src/` found **68 sites** selecting battlefield permanents off CR 613.1's printed *starting point* rather than the CR 613.5 computed answer; 63 fixed. The live consequences were not theory: `legal_actions` never offered an animated land as an attacker or blocker while `why_cannot_attack`/`why_cannot_block` allowed it (the engine permitted what the action list hid, which is exactly the asymmetry the bug report's "it blocked earlier in the same game" describes); "sacrifice a creature" and "tap an untapped creature you control" could not be paid with one; equip refused it as a host; CR 106.6's restricted mana refused its abilities; the legend rule could not see a clone's copied name or supertype. **The trap that decides whether the fix is right or catastrophic**: do NOT make `CardState.types` layer-aware. `CardState._asking_the_board` guards re-entrancy *per object*, so a board computation entered from A's `.types` reads B's unguarded `.types` and starts a whole nested board computation, which reads C — branching n deep, not merely recursive. Every fix is at the call site, and every loop over the battlefield hoists one `computed_board()` because `Game.characteristics_of` reruns the entire layer stack on every call by design (CR 613.5's "continually"). Two deferred cross-file items turned out to be **wrong to apply**: threading `computed_subtypes` into `triggers.activated_abilities_of` would have changed no answer (`layers.rewritten_ability_lines` and `added_ability_lines` already deliver Blood Moon's and Urborg's layer-4 mana abilities — verified live, `{T}: Add {R}` and `{T}: Add {G}`+`{T}: Add {B}` respectively) while breaking one, since that function is memoised on `(card.id, face)` alone and a per-permanent override would both outlive the subtype change and leak across every permanent sharing the printed card. Five sites stay printed with reasons, not excuses: `is_class`/`class_ability_text` are read from *inside* board computation (`layers.effects_for`, `quoted_ability_grants`) and hit the same blowup; the cast-spell trigger emitter and `ManaSpend.casting` name a spell on the stack, which `computed_board` does not cover at all; `is_snow_source` has no pool card granting a supertype in layer 4. `ManaSpend` gained a `HasCharacteristics` Protocol rather than a `Card | Characteristics` union, because `models` may not import `machines`. Separately recorded as a genuine gap rather than fixed: `Characteristics` carries `mana_value` but **no `mana_cost`**, so CR 700.5a's devotion-after-copy-effects cannot be right for a clone — `devotion_to` reading `CardState.mana_cost` is not a consumer bug, it is reading the only mana cost the engine has.
- One unknown word on a comma line deleted every keyword on it — 2026-09-09. Confirmed by reproduction before touching code: Emrakul, the Promised End's computed board read `has_keyword(flying)` and `has_keyword(trample)` as **False**, so a 3/3 ground creature could legally block a 13/13 flier and its trample damage reached nobody. Two files, one seam: `machines/combat._computed_keywords` built its reading vocabulary as combat's own `Keyword` list plus the single word being asked about, and `machines/abilities._parse_keywords` is all-or-nothing, so `protection from instants` - a word that vocabulary did not have - discarded flying and trample with it and the line fell through as an `UnparsedAbility` with nothing recorded. Six printings lost a printed combat keyword this way, and the same line answered two ways depending on the question: `has_keyword(Ketramose, "indestructible")` was True while `has_keyword(Ketramose, "menace")` was False, same card, same board, same line. The wrong assumption was written down at `layers._KNOWN_KEYWORDS` ("`has_keyword` adds the word it is asked about, so a granted line is found by whoever asks") - true only for a line holding nothing but that keyword. **The fix is the vocabulary, never the all-or-nothing rule**: `_reading_vocabulary` adds the permanent's own computed `CardState.keywords`, which is permission to *recognise* a word the computed text already says and grants nothing (a face-down permanent, CR 708.2, still contributes none and still has none). Two traps make relaxing `_parse_keywords` into a partial parse actively wrong, and both are real printings in the pool: Extinguisher Battleship's `5+ | Flying, trample` is CR 721.2a's station symbol, conditional on five charge counters, so keeping the `trample` a partial reader can match would be a fabricated unconditional grant; and Nevinyrral, Urborg Tyrant's `Hexproof from artifacts, creatures, and enchantments` is CR 702.11f shorthand for three abilities, of which a partial read keeps one. Both still fail closed, and now record a `KeywordLineNotice` naming the unread tokens instead of vanishing. Also not adding `protection`/`ward` to `combat.Keyword`: that enum is iterated at `layers._keyword_counter_lines` as CR 122.1b's grantable keyword counters, so a member there mints a counter this engine has no business minting. Schema bumped 4 -> 5, because a version-4 snapshot is a board where Emrakul has no flying.
- Cost/alternative-casting wave (Affinity, Improvise, Splice, Escape, Sunburst, Unearth, Transmute, Replicate, Fuse) — 2026-09-09. Three of the nine (Affinity, Improvise, Splice) were already fully built and tested; the sweep reported them ignored only because `scripts/sweep_pool.py`'s `executed_keywords` never asked `machines/objects_as_costs.py`/`Game.cost_reduction_for`/`Casting.splice` at all - the same "measured the wrong dispatcher" shape as the two entries above this one. Escape, Sunburst, Unearth, Transmute and Replicate each reused an existing shape rather than inventing one: Escape is flashback's `KeywordCastOption` with a different rule number (its "exile N **other** cards" additional cost needed one general fix - `machines/cost._requirement` had no reading for CR 201.4's self-exclusion word "other"); Sunburst is a keyword-triggered CR 614.1c "enters with" replacement read directly off `Card.keywords` rather than a parsed sentence, since its own CR 702.44a expansion is reminder text; Unearth/Transmute are `off_zone_abilities` entries built from `_synthetic_ability` the same way cycling already is; Replicate is `pay_kicker`'s "any number of times" branch plus `_storm_ability`/`_storm_effect`'s copy machinery with the count swapped from `spells_cast_this_turn` to `CastRecord.replicated`. **Fuse (CR 702.102) needed the only genuinely new plumbing**: `CardState.fused` plus `Casting.cast_fused`, and both `Casting.base_cost`/`.targeting` *and* `Game._resolve_top`/`._resolution_profile` had to grow a fuse-aware branch each, since a fused spell's cost, targets, and resolution are two independent faces combined rather than the single `up_face` every other multi-faced layout reads - missing the resolution-side `_resolution_profile` branch specifically would have let a fused spell's own CR 608.2b recheck silently refuse a legal second-half target. **Separately, and not part of this wave's own scope**: Summoner's Pact and Pact of Negation were reported fully silent by the sweep, but the delayed pay-or-lose-the-game mechanism (`machines/delayed.PayOrLoseTheGame`, `machines/effects._parse_delayed_pay_or_lose_clauses`) was already built and already passing `tests/scenarios/test_pact_delayed_upkeep_cost.py` end to end - `classify_clauses` was asking `trigger_effect_for` about a non-permanent spell's own multi-paragraph delayed-trigger-creating text as though it were a permanent's independent printed triggered ability, a dispatcher `Game._effect_of` never calls for anything but an `AbilityOnStack`. Fixed by gating that branch on `is_permanent`, mirroring the STATIC branch's own existing guard. **A `mise run test`/`uv run pytest` trap specific to this worktree**: a fresh worktree's `.venv` had no `pytest` installed (only `mise run install`'s own base venv creation had run), and `uv run pytest` silently fell back to resolving `pytest` from a *different* venv earlier on `PATH` - the sibling main checkout's - so tests ran against the wrong, unmodified copy of the engine with no error at all. `mise run install` (`uv sync --all-extras`) in the worktree is what fixes it. The cross-repo check has the identical trap one level up: `magic-cards-model-api`'s `pyproject.toml` pins `magic-cards-model = { path = "../magic-cards-model" }`, always the shared checkout, never a worktree - `uv pip install -e <worktree-path>` swaps it, but a plain `uv run` re-syncs from `pyproject.toml` and silently reverts the swap before every single command; `uv run --no-sync` is what keeps it pointed at the worktree.
- [Meld, Companion, and Paradigm](memory/meld-companion-paradigm-2026-09-09.md) — 2026-09-09. Meld's merged-object subsystem reuses transform's `up_face` idiom, with the partner card lifted off `Game.battlefield` into `Game.melded_pairs` rather than sweeping every battlefield-enumerating call site; Companion's condition parser shipped a real near-miss where the first draft assumed wording for Gyruda/Obosh/Yorion/Keruga that turned out not to match any real printing, caught by `resolve_cards` before shipping; Paradigm's "first time this game" needs no separate counter, since the unbounded delayed trigger's own presence already answers it
- ", and " is an Oxford comma as often as it is a CR 106.6 rider — 2026-09-09. Extending `machines/cost.py`'s spend-clause grammar to and-joined type lists ("Spend this mana only to cast instant and sorcery spells", Tablet of Discovery) tripped over `_SPEND_RIDER`, which cut the sentence at the first ", and " to drop Cavern of Souls'/Delighted Halfling's "and that spell can't be countered". Fíli and Kíli, Joyous prints "cast Dwarf, Equipment, and Saga spells", where those same five characters are a list separator, and cutting there silently dropped the last subtype - a restriction narrower than the card, which reads as a mysterious refusal rather than as a parse failure. Fix is ordering, not a smarter regex: read the whole phrase first and only try the rider cut when that fails, which is exactly when a rider is present, because a rider ends the sentence on a verb and no clause pattern can end there. The same ordering (whole phrase before splitting on " or ") is what lets "cast a Knight or Equipment spell" stay one clause while Eldrazi Temple's cast-or-activate sentence still becomes two. Two other traps in the same change: `_ZONE_WORDS` already existed 2800 lines further down in `cost.py` bound to four zones, so a same-named module-level dict for the new "from <zone>" tail was silently shadowed (renamed `_SPEND_ZONE_WORDS`); and a scenario that moved a card into exile by hand mid-test broke the conftest replay invariant, because `CardState.move_to` mints a `zone_change_stamp` outside the deterministic id scope - deal the card into exile with the scenario builder, or better, let the card's own enters trigger do it. A zone phrase naming an owner ("from your graveyard") is deliberately left unread: `ManaSpend` carries the origin zone and not whose it was, so reading it would produce a restriction quietly wider than the card.
- A colour pool is not a colour *choice* - 2026-09-09. `Action.color_options` alone made Interdimensional Web Watch's "Add two mana in any combination of colors" unreachable as a mixture for any caller driving the engine through `legal_actions`: a flat five-colour pool reads exactly like a dual land's, so only the five monochrome pairs could be named even though `Game.activate_ability(color=[...])` accepted `{W}{U}` all along. The missing half is the count, not the pool - `Game.mana_color_choice_count` / `Action.color_choice_count`, which counts *colours named* and so is 1 for Shang-Chi's "two mana of any one color" (two mana, one colour) and 0 whenever the pool is empty. Splitting it from `mana_color_options` rather than changing that return type kept every existing caller, the api repo's `ability_hints` included, working untouched.
- A cited card is evidence only if it exists - 2026-09-09. The comment on `machines/abilities._ANY_COLOR_COMBINATION` named "Gwenna, Smokebraider" as the second printing of "Add two mana in any combination of colors": no such card exists, and it conflated two names into one. The real second printing is **Smokebraider** ("{T}: Add two mana in any combination of colors. Spend this mana only to cast Elemental spells or activate abilities of Elementals."), now in the pool and played in `tests/scenarios/test_mana_spend_restrictions.py` - its `{G}{W}` casts Voice of Resurgence and refuses Llanowar Elves. Generality asserted in prose is worth nothing here; the rule is to add the real printing and play it, the same way Fíli and Kíli, Joyous proved the and-joined type list. Checking a card name costs one `curl` to `api.scryfall.com/cards/named?exact=`.
- A printed plural is read back through the whole subtype list, never by a suffix rule - 2026-09-10. Rules text names the objects ("activate abilities of Elementals") where a type line names one card ("Creature - Elemental Shaman"), so a CR 106.6 clause that kept the printed word matched nothing while `ManaRestriction.understood` still said True - the inverted version of the one signal that field exists to give. Magic's own type list sinks morphology (Elves/Elf, Mice/Mouse, Oxen/Ox, Fungi/Fungus beside Myr and Eldrazi, unchanged), so `magic_cards_model/data/subtypes.py` runs it the other way: every subtype WOTC has printed (Scryfall's catalogs via `scripts/add_subtypes.py`, the same list CR 205.3g-q prints) through the plural forms English gives a word of its shape, indexed, and a word that lands on none - or on two - resolves to nothing and fails the clause closed. The morphology then only ever runs on a word already known to be a subtype, so a wrong rule invents an unmatched plural instead of an unintended subtype
- The joining word decides whether printed qualities are alternatives or all required - 2026-09-10. A CR 106.6 clause asked for one of its card types AND one of its subtypes, which is two readings of one printed list applied at once: Séance Board's "cast instant, sorcery, Demon, and Spirit spells" is four alternatives and permitted nothing at all, while "artifact creature spells" is one object with two types and accepted a plain creature. A joined list ("instant and sorcery", "Aura and/or Equipment") is alternatives; juxtaposition ("Dragon creature", "colorless Eldrazi") is a conjunction; `SpendClause.joined` carries which, and a phrase that mixes them fails closed. The same three joining words also split two whole clauses - Woodland Weavemaster prints "and" where Eldrazi Temple prints "or"
- Chromatic Orrery does not print "five mana in any combination of colors" any more - 2026-09-10. Its current Oracle text is "You may spend mana as though it were mana of any color. / {T}: Add {C}{C}{C}{C}{C}." A review named it as the Modern-legal proof for counts past three; the real ones are Cascading Cataracts ("{5}, {T}: Add five mana in any combination of colors.", in the pool now) and Baxter Building's "four". Same lesson as the Smokebraider entry above, one step further on: a card that exists can still have been errata'd out of the wording the claim needs, so check the text and not only the name
- [The front face answered for the chosen half](memory/front-face-answered-for-the-chosen-half.md) — 2026-09-12. **The engine read the front face's type line to decide whether a half could be cast**, so a split card's timing came from whichever half was printed on the left: Replicate, the Sorcery half of Repudiate // Replicate (`Instant // Sorcery`), was announced during an **upkeep step** and went onto the stack, while Incongruity, the Instant half of Incubation // Incongruity (`Sorcery // Instant`), was refused during one. `Card.types` goes through `Card.front_type_line` and `targeting_profile(card)` through `Card.front_oracle_text` - neither answers CR 709.3a's "only the chosen half is evaluated to see if it can be cast", and neither does CR 709.4c's combined reading. Ask `types_being_cast`/`face_being_cast`, or read `CardState` (which goes through `up_face`). The ordering is the trap: CR 709.3/715.3 put the choice before the card goes onto the stack, but CR 601.2a's move happens in `Casting.model_post_init`, so re-asking the timing question inside `cast_as_split_half` reads a stack this very spell is already on and refuses a main-phase Sorcery half - the answer is frozen per face at the proposal (`Casting.face_cast_refusals`) and spent later instead
- [The enumeration must ask the announcement machine's own question](memory/enumeration-must-agree-with-announcement.md) — 2026-09-12. `Casting.targeting` and `Game._resolution_profile` both read a split card's chosen half; `Game._announcement_profile` was never taught, so `Action.target_slots` and `Game.legal_targets` reported the **front** half's targeting phrase on both rows of every split card - the Tear row of Wear // Tear advertising Wear's "target artifact", and the Incongruity row advertising no target at all for a spell CR 601.2c refuses without one. `legal_actions` had the matching shape of bug in the timing check: one `why_cannot_cast_now` call hoisted above its own loop over the halves answered for the wrong half every time. A per-variant question asked outside the per-variant loop is always wrong, and a candidate list built from a second read of the rule can always disagree with the slots it belongs to - build both from one `_announcement_profile` call. CR 715.3a/715.3b being CR 709.3a/709.3b word for word is why the same fix had to cover Adventure: scoping it to `Layout.SPLIT` left the identical rule unimplemented, and an Adventure announced against its whole printing resolved dealing nothing
- The same front-face read had a third instance, under CR 611.1 — 2026-09-12. `why_cannot_cast_restriction` read `spell.card.types` as well, so **Stomp was cast freely under Ranger-Captain of Eos's "Your opponents can't cast noncreature spells this turn"**, and again out of a graveyard under **Soulless Jailer's "Players can't cast noncreature spells from graveyards or exile"** - an `Instant - Adventure` is a noncreature spell however creature-like the card it is printed on. Three instances of CR 601.3e is what turned a pair of defects into a family, and why both CR 601.3 questions are now frozen on one dict under one umbrella (`Casting.face_cast_refusals`) and asked through one call (`legal_actions._why_cannot_cast_face`): fixing one and leaving the other hoisted above the per-face loop would have looked like a fix. The zone prohibition also supplied the other half of the ordering argument - CR 601.2a's move makes `spell.zone` the stack, so a zone question asked after it is asked about the wrong zone. `machines/casting_restrictions.py` cannot import `machines/casting.py`, so the CR 601.3e substitution is passed in by the caller that knows the face rather than re-derived there
- Known information is records plus two counters, not a visibility engine - 2026-09-13. `Reveal` gained `viewers`/`zone_change_stamp`/`turn`/`library_epoch`, `Game.look` records a CR 701.20e look, and `Game.known_to` is a pure reader that checks each record live: the stamp answers CR 400.7 and `Player.library_epoch` answers CR 701.20d, so nothing has to find and delete expired records at the moment they expire. The epoch lives on `Player.add` into a library (every put, including mulligan bottoming) plus `shuffle_library`/`Player.shuffle`/scry/surveil and `note_library_changed` at each direct `library.insert`/`append` - a new direct list write that skips it leaves a stale record live, so route it through `add` or call it. **The known-draw carry is an owner policy, not a CR rule**: a live library record follows its card straight from the owner's library into the owner's hand as a `KnownCarry` data row stamped with the new stamp, hooked in `Player.add` rather than `Game.draw_cards` because Goblin Guide, explore and look-top-N effects move library-to-hand without drawing and `CardState.move_to` has no game to ask. Schema 7 -> 8
- Typecycling and transmute auto-picked the first match in library order - 2026-09-13. `_cycling_effect` and `_transmute_effect` walked the library and moved the first matching card to hand without `Game.ask`, so no CHOOSE_CARDS question existed for a seat to answer (a live Islandcycling picked the Island for the player), nothing was revealed, the library was never shuffled, and "find nothing" (CR 701.23b) was impossible. Only the five basic land types plus landcycling/typecycling had `KEYWORD_EFFECTS` rows, so `Wizardcycling` and `Basic landcycling` resolved to nothing. Both now go through `Game.search_library(optional=True, shuffle=False)`, then `Game.reveal`, hand, shuffle; any "*cycling" keyword routes by shape via `keyword_activation_effect`, and `_matches_type` reads each named word as a supertype, card type or subtype (CR 702.29e "combination thereof")
- 2026-09-13: Duskwatch-family looks are recorded once, by `Game.look_at_top_and_split`, never by the caller (e3 and e4 each recorded one, so every card got two). "In any order" (CR 401.4) pins the look to the put-back epoch and stays known; "in a random order" passes `rest_in_random_order=True`, which pins it to the current epoch so the one put-back ends it (Narset, Thundertrap Trainer). The chosen card's public reveal is recorded after the CR 608.2d check and before `Player.add`, so the carry into hand sees a live record.
- A look-and-split's put-back knowledge is pinned to the epoch one reordering on, not recorded by its callers - 2026-09-13. `Game.look_at_top_and_split` hands both halves to its caller (Duskwatch-shape, explore, Devourer of Destiny), so the look for the cards put back cannot be recorded after the put-back without touching every caller; it is recorded at split time with `library_epoch = current + 1`, the epoch the caller's single `note_library_changed` produces. Sound only because every looked-at card is off the library list until the caller writes it back with its stamp unchanged: anything else (hand, graveyard, exile, `Player.add` restamping) fails closed. A new caller that writes cards back without moving the epoch exactly once would leave the record dormant until an unrelated reordering revives it - route it through `note_library_changed` once. Scry and surveil do their own put-back, so they record after it
- Hideaway's granted look is recorded when control arrives, not computed by `known_to` - 2026-09-13. CR 702.75a gives the look to whoever controls the permanent now, and CR 406.3 lets a player once allowed keep looking until the card leaves exile, so a computed "current controller" answer would drop a thief's knowledge the moment Threaten wears off. `Game.note_hideaway_viewers` is called from `_hideaway_effect` and from `ContinuousEffects.create`/`add` whenever a layer 2 effect is stored, which is the only post-entry control change this engine has; a future static control effect (an Aura's "You control enchanted creature") must call it too
- `Decision.source_id` names the object asking every question - 2026-09-13. Every `Decision(...)` and every call into a funnel that builds one (`Game.may`/`scry`/`search_library`/..., and the `machines/effects.py` helpers `_discard_cards`, `amass`, `learn`, ...) passes `source_id` explicitly, `None` only for rules-asked questions (mulligan, combat declarations, cleanup discard, legend rule, trigger ordering); `tests/test_decision_source_id_sites.py` fails on any site that omits it, so a new asker cannot forget. `ClauseEffect`, `ReferentClause` and `_SacrificeConsequence` gained a trailing `source_id` argument to carry it. A modal *spell* never asks `CHOOSE_A_MODE` (its modes are announced while casting); only a modal trigger does. Schema 7 -> 8. The typecycling/transmute fix above landed first on main; its new `search_library`/`reveal` call sites in `_cycling_effect`/`_transmute_effect` were extended to pass `source_id` too as part of this rebase.
- 2026-09-13: script-dsl (known information) and main (`Decision.source_id`) each bumped `Game.CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 7 -> 8 independently, so merging main into script-dsl moved it to 9: a version-8 dump from either line lacks the other line's fields, and a shared 8 would decode it as current. When two branches both bump, the merge bumps once more rather than keeping the shared number.
- 2026-09-13: impending's "this permanent's impending cost was paid" is `CardState.cast_with_keyword`, not `Game.cast_records`: the record is keyed by `CardState.id`, which survives a blink, so reading it would give a blinked Overlord its layer 4 "not a creature" and end step tick back (CR 400.7). `move_to` clears the field and `Game.enter_the_battlefield` sets it only when `was_cast`. Schema 9 -> 10; a sibling line bumping in parallel needs the same merge-to-next-number treatment as the 7 -> 8 note above.
- 2026-09-13: `Game.scry`/`surveil`/`look_at_top_and_split` record the chooser's look BEFORE asking (`_look_before_choosing`) and drop those records by identity at the put-back (`_end_looks`), where the existing post-put-back (or epoch+1) record replaces them. Replace, not add: tests and the api count one look per card, and a surveilled card binned would otherwise leave a dead record. Reason: the api's paused view reads the half-applied game, which held no record mid-choice (battle 1, D13 surveil).
- Mobilize resolved making nothing because `KEYWORD_TRIGGER_EFFECTS` had no entry - 2026-09-13. The trigger fell through to `trigger_effect_for` and logged only a delayed-clause notice. `_mobilize_effect` reads N (or X via `count_expression`, notice if uncountable) off the keyword line, asks CR 508.4's defender per token only when `attackable_defenders` offers two or more (`create_tokens` takes a per-token `attacking` chooser so the ask follows replacement effects), and one `SacrificeEach` delayed trigger sacrifices them all.
- The city's blessing waited for a state-based check - 2026-09-13. CR 702.131b says "any time" and CR 704.5 lists no ascend item, so Ocelot Pride never copied the Cat that was its own tenth permanent. `grant_the_citys_blessing` now runs at the start of `Game.emit` (before trigger matching, CR 702.131d) as well as in the SBA pass, and CR 702.131a spell ascend is followed in `_resolve_top` before the spell's text.
- Cast-trigger questions "before payment" (battle report B2-13) did not reproduce in the engine - 2026-09-13. Paused mid-cast, Ugin's target and Devourer's order question are raised after CR 601.2h with the spell on the stack and every land tapped; the api served its committed snapshot during a pause, which showed the pre-cast board; magic-cards-model-api now serves the paused board for announcement pauses (`commands.paused_board`, `tests/test_paused_cast_board.py`). Guard tests live in `test_cast_triggers.py`.
- A printed "reveal it" on a search is a real reveal, recorded by one funnel - 2026-09-13. `_SEARCH_AND_PUT_RE` read and discarded "reveal it" on the stale belief that showing a card changes no tracked state, so Expedition Map's and Ranger-Captain of Eos's finds were never known to the opponent (battle 2, B2-04). Every search-reveal-put template (the singular search with "reveal it"/"reveal that card", the referent search, "up to N ... reveal them", Beseech's board-count bound, the conditional consequence, typecycling and transmute) now goes through `Game.search_and_put(..., reveal=...)`: search without shuffling, reveal each find (CR 701.23e, 701.20a), put, then shuffle. The shuffle has to come after the put: `carry_knowledge_into_hand` needs the library epoch unchanged, and a shuffle before the put ends the record before it can be carried. Non-reveal searches take the same printed order, which does not move the shuffle's seed. Trinket Mage's "reveal that card" did not match the regex at all, so it had silently done nothing.
- The known-draw carry now also covers outside-the-game-to-hand - 2026-09-13. Karn's -2 recorded its reveal against `Zone.SIDEBOARD` and then moved the card, so CR 400.7 ended the record in the same resolution and the opponent never knew the card (B2-05), although docs/engine-coverage.md claimed it worked. `Player.add` now carries from any hidden group its owner holds (library or `zone.is_outside_the_game`) and passes the origin zone; a non-library record needs `library_epoch is None`. This is the same owner policy as E1, widened to cover the move that follows a public reveal. The Burning Wish family was unparsed (it resolved silently) and now shares `_reveal_and_bring_into_hand` with Karn. "Exile <this>" exiles the popped resolving spell, and `resolve_top` skips the graveyard for a spell already in exile.
- A bottom put keeps the records of the cards it did not move - 2026-09-13. This decision replaces E1's blanket "every insertion moves the epoch" for bottom puts (B2-15: a second scry that bottomed two cards expired Devourer's record, although Devourer never moved). CR 701.20d ends only "revealed cards that are reordered", and CR 401.2's pile keeps every other card in its order when a card goes under all of them, or when a scry or surveil takes its own cards off the top. The epoch design stays coarse: `Player.note_library_changed(placed=[...])` moves the epoch and `Game.keep_untouched_library_records` appends a `LibraryOrderKept` row (card, stamp, new epoch) to `Game.library_orders_kept` for each untouched card with a live library record, and `Game._library_order_holds` reads a record as live when every epoch since it was made has a row for that card and stamp (data, so `known_to` stays pure). Two rejected cuts: appending re-pinned `Reveal` copies broke `reveals` being one entry per showing (the Charbelcher, Mosswort Bridge and Watcher for Tomorrow tests count them), and rewriting `Reveal.library_epoch` in place broke its meaning, the epoch the card was shown at (the Charbelcher and Atraxa tests pin it). Schema 9 -> 10, then 10 -> 11 on landing beside impending. No `placed` (a shuffle, a put on top, explore or Devourer keep-on-top, a cost rewind) still ends everything, and top insertion still ends records as E1's Subtlety test says. `put_cards_into_library`'s top path now calls `note_library_changed()` itself after its lift, because `add` no longer voids. The old mulligan test asserting that bottoming ends the top card's look was the over-expiry, and it is replaced. Records pinned one epoch ahead by `look_at_top_and_split` are untouched by the re-pin, so its "move the epoch exactly once" contract holds.
- 2026-09-13: impending (battle-fixes) and G1b's `Game.library_orders_kept` each bumped `Game.CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 9 -> 10 independently, so landing G1b on battle-fixes moved it to 11, per the 7 -> 8 merge note above.
- Learn's Lesson half and Glittering Wish share the outside-the-game reveal - 2026-09-13. `learn` did only CR 701.48a's discard-to-draw half, still claiming the engine had no outside-the-game group, so Divide by Zero, Eyetwitch and First Day of Class never revealed a Lesson. It now offers the Lesson reveal whenever no card was discarded (declined, or an empty hand), and it goes through `_reveal_and_bring_into_hand`, so the Lesson is carried as known into hand. Glittering Wish resolved to nothing because `_search_quality_is_readable` rejected "multicolored". CR 105.2a/b color counts now live in `_COLOR_COUNT_WORDS`, beside `_HAND_EXILE_COLOR_WORDS` rather than in it, because that dict maps a word to a single color and Ugin's Labyrinth reads it too. A search printing "multicolored" or "monocolored" now reads through the same matcher.
- 2026-09-13: "Choose a creature type" validates against CR 205.3m's own list, bundled as `data/creature_types.json` by `scripts/add_creature_types.py` from `.rules/MagicCompRules.txt` - deliberately not `subtypes.json`'s creature group, which is Scryfall's catalog of printed subtypes (350 entries vs the rules' 324) and so a different answer to a question the rules define. `Decision.quality` carries which quality was asked so the refusal names the rule instead of listing 324 words; the rules' typographic apostrophes (C'tan, Shi'ar) are stored straight to match Oracle type lines.
- 2026-09-13: Malevolent Rumble lost its `EFFECTS` row to `_merge_reveal_top_to_hand_rest_to_graveyard_clauses` (a clause-run merger, not a whole-text fullmatch, so Rumble's trailing Spawn sentence keeps `_parse_token_clause`). The reveal must be recorded before any card moves: `Player.add`'s known-draw carry only sees a live library record, so revealing after `player.move` to hand would leave the card unknown - the exact B3-03 symptom. "permanent card" (CR 110.4a) is now a `_quality_matcher` word; before it matched nothing.
- 2026-09-13: A look-and-split `Decision.amount` is the number of cards the player may pick, not the number looked at (`len(Decision.cards)`), and `Game.ask` enforces it for SCRY/SURVEIL. Scry and surveil had to become `exact=False` in the same change, or enforcing `exact` would refuse every legal scry; an exactly-N split (Waker of Waves, Codecracker Hound) now defaults to the first N cards, since its old empty default was refused by its own effect.
- 2026-09-13: CR 401.4's bottom order is its own question (`Game.ask_library_bottom_order`, an ordered `CHOOSE_CARDS`), not `CardSplit.top_order` reused: a seat reading "top_order" for cards going to the bottom was the B3-14 report. The funnel only asks - placement and the CR 701.20d epoch stay with each caller, whose put-back already records knowledge correctly. Cards off every zone while asked get `card_labels` from the asker, since `_with_card_labels` cannot find them.
- 2026-09-13: Tokens had `owner=None` because `TokenState(...)` was built with `controller=` only at all four construction sites in `models/token.py` (stated, extra from a replacement, copy, copy's extra); `Player.add`'s owner stamp never runs for a token, which goes straight to the battlefield. CR 111.2 makes the creator the owner. No schema bump: the field already existed.
- 2026-09-13: The CR 401.4 bottom-order question needed no engine change to resume: it is an ordinary `Game.ask` CHOOSE_CARDS, so the tape replays it positionally after the look split (seq 0 split, seq 1 order) and card ids stay stable through `model_validate`. Proven by pausing, storing the `PendingDecision` through `TypeAdapter` JSON, and re-running from the JSON snapshot. A live-vs-replay dump differs only in `decisions` (tape vs script bookkeeping), so compare with `exclude={"decisions"}`.
- 2026-09-13: Battle 3 landed on #14 as schema 11 -> 12, overriding the token-owner line's "no schema bump": `Decision.quality` is a new field reachable from the dump (`DecisionScript.asked`), token `owner` now has a value, and Malevolent Rumble's `EFFECTS` row became a parser - each a bump-rule bullet on its own.
- A reveal-from-hand cost is `ComponentKind.REVEAL`, and its X crosses the two activation calls through `Game.announced_reveal_counts` - 2026-09-13. The api calls `begin_activating` and `activate_ability` separately, each with its own `x_value` (0 when unset), so an X fixed by the number of cards revealed has nowhere else to live; `activate_ability` pops it. `cost_announces_x` (any X in a cost, CR 107.3a) replaced `"{X}" in cost` at every gate, and `activated_effect_for` now tries the X-substituted text first: the bare text parsed "+X/+X" as a layer effect with no value behind it.
- Martyr of Spores, Frost and Bones were dropped as the X-reveal proof - 2026-09-13. Each exposed a gap outside battle 5 (a pump whose source was sacrificed does nothing; an activated counter-unless-pay is unread; "from a single graveyard" is unread), now rows in Not modelled. Martyr of Ashes proves the template; Thief of Existence was dropped from the target-phrase proof for its unread "If you do, ... gains" rider, replaced by Witherbloom Command.
- A search description is split on "or a"/"or an", never on a bare "or" - 2026-09-13. The article is what separates two whole descriptions (Starfield Shepherd) from a type list inside one ("a Mountain or Plains card"). An unreadable description fails closed with `SearchDescriptionNotice` instead of matching nothing; readability accepts printed subtypes through `subtype_vocabulary`, because `has_land_type` accepts any string.
- Modal trigger modes are chosen in `_put_pending_triggers_on_stack`, not at resolution - 2026-09-13. The resolution-time choice let CR 603.3d's whole-text target announcement remove Abiding Grace when its return mode had no target (B5-08). "One or both" is several `CHOOSE_A_MODE` questions with `NO_FURTHER_MODES` offered first once the minimum is met, so an unscripted player takes the minimum and the api needs no new decision kind.
- A card moved from a public zone into its owner's hand is known to every player (owner policy, B5-04) - 2026-09-13. Three existing tests asserted the opposite (a bounce, a face-up exile return, Karn's face-up exile pick) and were updated to the policy. Face-down exile carries nothing (CR 406.3).
- Skyclave Apparition's link is `ExileMarker.LINKED`, set by the generic exile-target effect only when the source prints a "the exiled card"/"exiled with" reader (CR 607.2a) - 2026-09-13. CR 607.3 sums a variable over several linked cards, so the Illusion is one token per owner with X summed, not one per card.
- 2026-09-13: J1 bumped `Game.CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 11 -> 12 for `AbilityOnStack.modes`/`mode_targets`, `Game.announced_reveal_counts`, `Game.search_description_notices`, `CostComponent.variable_count`/`source` and `ExileMarker.LINKED`. H1 (battle 3) landed first with its own 11 -> 12, so J1 landed as 12 -> 13 per the 7 -> 8 note; a 12 from either line lacks the other's fields.
- `sweep_pool.py` treats a mode's flavor-word label as no keyword (CR 207.2d) - 2026-09-13. Scryfall lists "Heckle" and "Hero Worship" in Flash Thompson's `keywords`; the keyword report now skips a word used only as a bullet label, which also cleared Scout the City's and Fire Magic's labels. `_x_candidates` uses `cost_announces_x`, mirroring the engine, so the Martyrs sweep as handled.
- 2026-09-13: Urza's Saga's "with mana cost {0} or {1}" was folded into "mana value 1 or less" on the belief the sets are identical; they are not (CR 202.1 vs 202.3e: {X}, {W} and no mana cost all pass the mana-value bound). `_exact_mana_cost_matcher` compares printed symbols. Only one printing uses the template, so generality is proven on the searched cards (Swooping Lookout, Seat of the Synod, Endless One).
- 2026-09-13: "You may cast that card this turn" after "Choose target <quality> card in your graveyard" (Emry, Silas Renn) is the permission pair with a chosen-target antecedent; `_PLAY_PERMISSION_RE` now takes either duration wording (CR 514.2 ends both). The orphan notice regex was widened in step, and now covers spells too, so an unread antecedent is never silent.
- 2026-09-13: Warp's delayed exile keyed on the keyword, and the from-exile cast is recorded under the same keyword; `CastRecord.keyword_cost_paid` (from the option's `pays_mana_cost`) is what "if this spell's warp cost was paid" reads. Do not key it on origin zone: a future grant could cast a warp card from hand for its mana cost under the keyword.
- 2026-09-13: Game over is enforced at the entry points (`_refuse_if_over`), not by aborting a resolution in progress: an effect that ends the game mid-resolution finishes reading defaults, since `Game.ask` returns `default_answer` unrecorded once `is_over`. `pass_priority` returns the new `PriorityOutcome.GAME_OVER` when its own pre-pass check ends the game; an api switch on the outcome must handle it.
- 2026-09-13: "Unless ... pays {N}" now counts untapped mana sources (CR 608.2g). The PAY_MANA answer stays a bool on the tape unless it names sources (`Answer.card_ids` beside `accept=True`), so existing tapes replay. Four `test_counterspells.py` scenarios had assumed floating-only payment with untapped lands on the board; they now tap Alice's lands as staging. The api's refusal text quoting CR 608.2g for a mid-payment activation lives in magic-cards-model-api `commands.py`, not here.
- 2026-09-13: Schema 12 -> 13 for `CastRecord.keyword_cost_paid` and `Decision.mana_sources`; concurrent battle-4/5 bumps need the merge-to-next-number treatment.
- 2026-09-13: The orphan cast-permission notice stays ability-only. Opening it to spells put an `UnmodeledTriggerEffectNotice` on Ragavan, Ugin, Tablet of Discovery and Transcendent Dragon, because `effect_for` (and the sweep's [spell] pass) is handed a permanent's whole printed text, where the sentence belongs to a triggered or activated ability. Measured with a per-card `classify_card` diff against the base commit, not the totals, which the new printings move.
- 2026-09-13: Game over broke five existing tests that resolved or kept priority after the game had ended (Toph/Caged Sun's draw, the Millennium Calendar's second trigger, Bolt with Bob already at 0, the second lethal Bolt, and Gut/Harrier's unpaid {2}); each asserted the pre-CR 104.1 or floating-only behaviour and was updated, not exempted. `retain_priority` stays a no-op rather than a refusal once the game is over, since it follows the action that ended it. Three tests had pinned the B6-10 wording itself ("needs a artifact").
- 2026-09-13: K1 (battle 6) rebased onto J1 (battle 5): both stamped 12 -> 13, so K1 landed as 13 -> 14. The exact-cost matcher moved into J1's `_search_descriptions` (per description, so an "or a" half keeps its own cost bound), and J1's first-letter `_with_article` now goes through `phrases.with_indefinite_article`.
- 2026-09-13: Review round 1 on K1: the pregame seat actions (declare_mulligan, take_opening_hand_action, finish_opening_hand_actions) were missing the game-over guard, reachable because CR 104.3a lets a player concede during the mulligan step. CardRequirement's subtype refusal and the two animation descriptions in layers.py bypassed with_indefinite_article ("not a Ally", Jolrael's substituted X giving "becomes a 8/8"); no real printing reaches the self-animation builder with a vowel-sound P/T, so it shares the fix but is proved through the target builder.
- 2026-09-13: "counters ... equal to <expr>" goes through one reader, `machines/effects.amount_expression` (numbers, X, "twice", `count_expression`/devotion values, "minus"/"plus", clamped at 0 by CR 107.1b), for both the enters-with replacement and the resolving effect. Slumbering Trudge's "three minus X" had parsed into `amount_expression` for months and was skipped silently because the old one-row resolver table knew only Ulamog's phrase; the table is gone. An unreadable expression now records `UnreadableCounterAmountNotice` (battle 4, B4-01). X inside an expression with no spell behind the object is 0 (CR 107.2), unlike the bare "enters with X counters" path, which still skips.
- 2026-09-13: a mana ability's CR 405.6c rider was applied only by `Game.activate_ability`; `CostPayment.tap_for_mana` (hand-picked sources and `auto_tap`) added the mana alone, so every pain source tapped for a spell was free (B4-03). Both now call `Game.apply_mana_ability_rider`. A new route that adds mana from an ability must call it too.
- 2026-09-13: combat damage assignment is a decision now (`ASSIGN_COMBAT_DAMAGE`, B4-06). The current CR 510.1c has no damage assignment order: without trample any division among blockers is legal; only CR 702.19b's damage past the blockers needs lethal on each first. `Decision.default_damage` keeps the old split for unscripted seats. The api has to learn `Answer.damage` before a live seat can pause on it. Schema 11 -> 12 on its own line; landed after battles 3 (H1), 5 (J1) and 6 (K1) as 14 -> 15, per the 7 -> 8 note.
- 2026-09-13: ward never asked (B4-07): `WardTrigger.begin_paying` existed but no game path called it, so every unpaid ward countered. `_ward_effect` asks through `Game.may_pay_unless_cost`, which keeps `PAY_MANA`/`PAY_LIFE` for pure mana/life costs and asks one `MAY` plus a `CHOOSE_CARDS` per discard/sacrifice part otherwise. Default policy is unchanged: an unscripted seat pays whenever CR 118.3 allows, the same as `PAY_LIFE`/`PAY_MANA` elsewhere.
- 2026-09-13: on landing after K1 (battle 6), five ward tests (`test_ward.py`, `test_ability_on_stack_round_trip.py`) that assumed an unpaid ward failed: K1 lets an "unless ... pays" `PAY_MANA` count untapped mana sources (CR 608.2g), and B4-07 now asks ward through it, so the unscripted caster, holding two untapped Mountains, paid Archive Dragon's {2}. The engine was right on both counts; the tests now decline with `will_pay_the_mana_cost(False)`, which is what "unpaid" meant.
- 2026-09-13: B4-04 (reveal before choosing) landed on H1's reveal-to-hand template, not a card function: on rebase `malevolent_rumble` was dropped for `_merge_reveal_top_to_hand_rest_to_graveyard_clauses`, which already records every reveal before its `Game.may(cards=...)`. `test_reveal_before_the_choice.py` passes against the template unchanged, and Commune with the Gods (off-pool, same three sentences, five cards, "creature or enchantment") proves it for every card sharing the template. Noted, not done here: Vein Ripper's "Whenever a creature dies" lands in the sweeper's can-never-fire bucket, the known `condition_matches` DIES gap.
- 2026-09-13: a negative life total refused every mana payment, even one with no Phyrexian symbol: `ManaPool.payment_for` rejects an option whose life exceeds `life_available`, and 0 > -1. It was unreachable until the payment-path rider (a Tarnished Citadel tapped at 2 life drops the payer to -1 mid-payment); the auto-tap property test found it on a random board. `CostPayment.life_available` and the auto-tap solver now clamp at 0 (CR 119.4b). The loss is still CR 704.5a's state-based action afterwards.
- Activation rows carry `target_slots`, with candidates, built by the engine - 2026-09-13. The api (#32, B5-10) filled them from the private `Game._announcement_profile`; now `legal_actions` builds every slot (cast, battlefield activation, loyalty, off-zone, and a new equip row) through one helper, `_target_candidates`, which applies `TargetRequirement.rejects` plus `protection_refusal` - the per-target half of `TargetingProfile.check`. `TargetSlot` gained `candidates` (ids) and `player_candidates` (usernames) for casts too, so both kinds of row share one shape. `Game.announcement_profile` is the public name, `_announcement_profile` an alias. A cast leaves its own card out (CR 115.5); an activation does not, since the ability is a different object from its source. Equip had no row at all (it is a keyword, absent from `Game.activated_abilities`): `_equip_action` builds one from `keywords.unannounced_equip_activation`, keyword "Equip", no `ability_index`. `equip_activation` now checks its target at announcement (CR 601.2c via 602.2b), so the two `test_equip.py` tests that expected an opponent's creature or an unanimated Sol Ring to be accepted and fizzle now expect a refusal. An off-zone build gets the first legal candidates per requirement minimum, so Boseiju with a target is priced; with none it stays UNKNOWN. No schema bump: `Action` is never part of a `Game` dump. Two api consequences on the next pointer advance: `action_rows.equip_rows` duplicates the engine's equip row and builds `equip_activation` with the Equipment as its own target, which now refuses; and `with_target_slots` becomes redundant.
- Activation targets are announced by `begin_activating`, before the cost is paid - 2026-09-13. CR 602.2b runs CR 601.2c before 601.2h, so `begin_activating(targets=...)` asks for or checks them and keeps them in a non-persisted `Game._announced_targets` (PrivateAttr: both calls run inside one command, and a paused command replays from its start), which `activate_ability` takes back without rechecking; CR 608.2b is what finds a sacrificed target gone. A persisted field would have needed a schema bump for state no snapshot ever holds. `activate_ability` still announces for a caller that skipped `begin_activating`.
- 2026-09-13: M1e review: `_announced_targets` now stores the `CostPayment` beside the targets and `activate_ability` honours the entry only when that payment is PAID. A rewound or abandoned `begin_activating` otherwise left its targets for a later direct `activate_ability` of the same ability to take unchecked. A JSON round trip between the two calls still drops the entry by design (no caller serializes mid-command; a pause replays from the start).
- 2026-09-13: Battle 7 B7-01: Expressive Iteration's parser read `player.library[:3]` and `Player.add`ed the hand card without taking it off the library, so one object sat in two zones. `Player.add` now lifts a card out of the list of the own (non-shared) zone it is leaving (CR 400.7), fixing the whole class, and `tests/scenarios/conftest.py` asserts `objects_in_several_zones` is empty after every `Game.resolve_top` and on every final game. Run on the unfixed tree, that invariant flagged only Expressive Iteration's own test, so no other effect had the shape.
- 2026-09-13: Triggered mana abilities (B7-02/B7-03) resolve inline on both paths and never through `_resolve_ability`: its `_before_priority` put the *second* waiting trigger (Badgermole Cub beside Leyline) on the stack and ran state-based actions mid-payment. The mana goes to `Game.mana_pool_receiving`, the open payment's pool, which is what lets it pay the same cast and lets CR 733.1's rewind drop it. The payment search sees it by extending each candidate option with `triggered_mana_bonus`; a plan entry lists the ability's own mana first and `tap_for_mana` strips the trigger's colours back off (`_activated_mana_ability_with_triggers`) and hands them over as `color_hints`, so a pinned `(source, [G, U])` and the auto-tapper both pick a Fertile Ground colour without a question. `triggers_for` was split into the side-effect-free `abilities_triggered_by` so the search never touches `once_per_turn_triggers` or mints ids.
